====================================================================================================
QUESTION: If the employee is diagnosed with a terminal illness and accelerates 75% of the death benefit, how much death benefit will the beneficiaries ultimately receive?
CATEGORY: CAT4_MULTIHOP_REASONING
EXPECTED ANSWER: The beneficiaries will receive only 25% of the original death benefit, since any amount accelerated reduces the death benefit payable to beneficiaries.
EVIDENCE: 7.1 If you are diagnosed with a terminal illness with life expectancy of 12 months or less, you may request acceleration of up to 75% of your death benefit. / 7.4 Any amount accelerated reduces the death benefit payable to beneficiaries.
====================================================================================================

--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: If an employee with a terminal illness accelerates 75% of the death benefit, what percentage of the death benefit do the beneficiaries ultimately receive?
reasoning: The query asks for the final amount of death benefit received by beneficiaries when an employee with a terminal illness accelerates 75% of the death benefit. This is a straightforward calculation based on a known percentage of the total death benefit. However, the total death benefit amount is not specified in the query. Since the question is asking for the ultimate amount beneficiaries receive after acceleration, and no specific total benefit value is provided, the answer must be expressed as a percentage of the original death benefit. The beneficiaries will receive the remaining 25% of the death benefit after 75% is accelerated. The query does not require external facts or multi-hop reasoning—only a simple arithmetic deduction based on the given percentage.
